The halves (images 191 and 192, VS R at x 206) hold 150 ticks, then VS L plays KGT sound 12 (PC 863) and they slide apart over 23 (dx ±50 to ±440). V (193 at 300, 140) and S (194 at 270, 280) hold 140 ticks, then fade by 10 and 20 of 32 (op `0x23` mode 4, PCs 934–937) for 5 each. From round 2 it spawns demo black (117, PC 76) at z `0x7f`: the logo (182) for 110 ticks, then 115's wipe. Their op `0x1f` loops wait on global 15 = 10 or global 10 = 10, which 13 and the select screen clear. 13's own displays only repeat its objects, so they aren't drawn: the halves joined (199, PCs 87–89) is VS L's and VS R's images where they stand, for 50 of their 150 still ticks, and the logo (182, PC 77) is demo black's first, held 10 of its 50. V and S flash in, op `0x23` adding 32, 20, then 10 to each 5-bit colour channel (PCs 916–922); the KO lettering's offsets (−1, 7, 7, then −3, 5, 10) tint it blue under its blend.
- **CPU:** routine `0x411270` (control type 1, case `0x4112f9`) plays the CPU fighter's own table: 100 slots of 111 bytes after the held-pose table, 43 used for Ryuko, 44 for Zoma, 43 for Kiki, 40 for Chung, 37 for Amiro, 45 for ANMC P, 43 for Giant, 41 for G Suneo, 41 for Yo and 42 for SIN. Each entry holds flags (bit 0 self airborne, bit 1 opponent airborne), a probability, a distance window in source pixels, and up to ten steps of direction, command and duration. While no entry plays, a timer counts down. At zero it resets to `rand % (101 − L) − L + 50`, every slot draws `rand % 100`, and the last entry that passes its probability, distance and airborne checks plays. Each step lasts its duration plus `rand % (101 − L)` ticks, plus one. The routine runs at the start of its fighter's step (`0x411867`, in `0x411810` before the landing check and the command scan), which a step entering a queued action, counting down a stop or held by op `0x07` (`+0x40` at −1, set at `0x41303a` until op `0x14` clears it) skips (`0x412327`, `0x412390`, `0x412397`): its timers then hold and draw nothing, and its input word that tick is player 2's keys as `0x4146d0` recorded them, none. It clears its input history each time (`0x403300`) and writes the step's direction, plus, for a command step, the command's whole input with that direction added (`0x411644`), so a command repeats through its step and entries such as down + punch attack crouching. When a hit reaches the CPU free (`+0x15e & 0xc` is 0: standing, walking, crouching, turning, or in a jump from takeoff), `0x40f3a2` guards on `rand % 100 < L`, ignoring its input (`0x40f3c2`); in a guard reaction (12) it keeps guarding, whichever way it faces (`0x40f449`). Ryuko's and Zoma's `+0x7cb6` (2) has bit 2, which lets that guard happen in the air (`0x40f475`). A CPU attacking, dashing, double jumping, falling after an air attack or landing (4), or reeling (8) takes the hit without a roll. Command 22 enters CPU IORI: action 410 (damage 10) jumps to 411 (damage 15). Op `0x20` at PC 5887 then recovers one time in six (`rand % 6 > 4`: 411 + 31, three 5-tick poses) and otherwise jumps to IORI 3 (248). Its op `0x1e` records have mode 0, which the cancel check at `0x41193a` ignores, so it cannot be cancelled. Zoma's CPU writes each command itself, as the engine does, every tick of its step, with the step's direction in every word (`Input.command`, injected by `Match.record`), since his CPU-only entries have no keys: 22 CPU DASHF (341: a dash at raw 680, then 200), 23 CPU DASHB (342), whose only CPU entry needs him airborne where it has no air action, so it never plays, and 24 cpu dash jump (343), the dash's start jumping into HIGH JUMP (6). Their steps end in button `0x40`, which no player can press. `rand` is MSVC's (`0x417a22`). Authored: level 50 (the story's value is not recovered), a separate seed per match, Ryuko's commands as the port's single buttons pressed on alternate ticks, and the neutral states (those where the player's own guard would start). Zoma's intro follows Ryuko's path: action 28 jumps to 30 + 3 for global 3 = 0 (PCs 265, 301, past its 50-tick display), which spawns Black and OP super Charge (36, PCs 303–304), charging two stocks, and jumps to Start N (31) + 2 (PC 306), as Ryuko's 32 + 3 jumps to 33 + 4. Start N shows 11 displays over 200 ticks with voices 19 and 20 (PCs 312, 320), but no op `0x01`, so he starts 180 pixels from the stage centre, where Ryuko's walk ends, and no op `0x16`, so holding down starts no EX mode.
- **Scope:** ten fighters, 18 stages, local single-player. No full gameplay VM, original-game parity, gamepad, multiplayer, or public deployment.

## Recovered contact timing

A strike's active/inactive cycle uses **action ticks**. Successful contact adds **hit-stop**: the fighter's action age, airborne age, position, velocity, and acceleration stop advancing for the specified ticks. This is separate from the victim's subsequent hit/block recovery. One tick is 10 ms in this port. Whiffs add no pause. Effects, the round clock and the input history continue, so chain and cancel presses made during the pause count once it ends, while still in their windows. After a KO or time-out the round phase stays 1 (only a debug key sets 2, `0x406231` and `0x408f38`): moves, objects, commands and the CPU run on, and each fighter takes its result's action once back in neutral (`0x410ff1`); only the timer stops (`0x408e50`).

| Attack                                                     | Successful-hit pause per strike              | Block pause          | Source evidence                                                                          |
| ---------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------- | -------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Punch 1 / Kick 1                                           | 10 ticks                                     | 10                   | Responses 1–2 → actions 73–74                                                            |
| Punch 2                                                    | 10, 10, 15                                   | 10                   | Action 180, PCs 2400/2413; responses 8–11                                                |
| Punch 3 / Kick 2 / crouching punch / aerial punch and kick | 15                                           | 10                   | Actions 181/188/185/182/191; strong/launch responses                                     |
| Hit Up                                                     | Ground: attacker 33, victim 10; air: 13 each | 10                   | Action 200; response 19 → 81, airborne response 17 → 80                                  |
| IORI                                                       | 15, 15, then ground 13 / air 15              | 10                   | Actions 246–248; responses 3/4, 71, 18/77 → 75/76, 121, 80/123                           |
| Crouching kick                                             | 13                                           | 10                   | Response 7 → action 79 (sweep)                                                           |
| Ground Drill Punch                                         | 15 for strikes 1–5; 13 for strike 6          | 10                   | Action 207, PCs 2920/2941; responses 10–11 → 75–76, 18 → 80                              |
| Kick 3                                                     | 0                                            | 0                    | Action 190, PC 2631; responses 20–21 → 83–84, 34–36 → 163/165/167                        |
| Aerial drill                                               | Attacker 8, victim 9                         | Attacker 8, victim 3 | Actions 209–211 contact branches, PCs 2989/3000, 3019–3020, 3040–3041, 3061–3062         |
| Axe object                                                 | 15 (FB → 75), airborne 15; Ryuko too         | 10                   | Action 227, PC 3288; responses 50 → 110 → 75, 4 → 76, 25–27                              |
| EX Fire shots                                              | 15 (FB → 75), full charge 13; Ryuko too      | 10                   | Objects 233/240; responses 50 → 110 → 75, 4 → 76, 58 → 112 → 80, 25–27                   |
| Getter Beam and super beam objects                         | Victim 0, finish 13; Ryuko and her beams too | 0                    | Objects 269/282/303/324 and EFS's 336/337; responses 51 → 111 → 83, 58 → 112 → 80, 34–36 |
| Super grab finish and hidden-super strikes                 | 13 each, or 0 for rapid strikes              | 10                   | Response 43 → 97, 17/18 → 80, 20 → 83; guards 78–80 → 168–172                            |

Each pause comes from the selected reaction's own `0x1a`, so stance-dependent responses (overheads, air guards) change the pause with the reaction. Aerial drill's hit and guard handlers enter the tick after contact and stop it eight ticks, the last of which moves, then two more ticks reach the next stage's box: 11 ticks per stage. Its victim resumes at different times on hit versus block, so subsequent contacts depend on their relative positions, not a fixed hit count.

Ground drill still strikes at action ages 23, 29, 35, 41, 47, 53. With every strike connecting immediately, the actual contact gaps are now **210 ms on hit** or **160 ms on block**, versus **60 ms for Kick 3**. Punch 2's first two gaps are 160 ms, and aerial kick's are 230 ms. The final strike's pause delays recovery rather than adding another strike.

Evidence: the character container tail contains 23 command entries of 82 bytes, followed by 83 four-byte response mappings at file offset `0x1576d9d`. Opcode `0x17` selects a mapping, and reaction opcode `0x1a` adds its first two bytes to the self/other fighter stop counters. The original engine handles this at `0x413637`/`0x4136ae`; `0x412390` skips animation advancement and `0x40f963` skips motion while stopped. The KGT base hit/guard stop bytes loaded at `0x43a299`/`0x43a29a` are both zero. This is static recovery from the supplied files, not original Windows playback verification. The other stop reaches every other player, so an object's hit stops its owner as a body hit does. Both stops also reach each stopped player's objects that follow it (op `0x04` flag `0x20`, `+0x28` bit `0x20000000`: Ryuko's beams 269, 282 and 324 and their glows, Zoma's 211, 242 and 245), which skip their script step and motion but keep to their parent (`0x40f992`); other objects, like the axe, run on.
